Combining Proposition
5.5
with (
5.7
) we see that

\displaystyle Q_{1}^{(n)}-Q_{1}^{(n+1)}=\tilde{Q}_{1}^{(n)}-\tilde{Q}_{1}^{(n+1)}+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}\log^{2}n}\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{Q_1^{(n)} - Q_1^{(n+1)}}{}=\lim_{z\to\infty}z\big(R^{(n)}(z)-R^{(n+1)}(z)\big)N(z)+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}\log^{2}n}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right).

(5.9)

Using Propositions
3.6
and
5.5
, and substituting in (
5.8
) and (
5.9
), we see that

\displaystyle b_{n-1}^{2}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}^{2}=\big(\big(Q_{1}^{(n)}\big)_{12}-\big(\tilde{Q}_{1}^{(n)}\big)_{12}\big)\big(\big(Q_{1}^{(n)}\big)_{21}-\big(Q_{1}^{(n+1)}\big)_{21}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{b_{n-1}^2 - \tilde{b}_{n-1}^2 =}{}+\big(\tilde{Q}_{1}^{(n)}\big)_{12}\big[\big(Q_{1}^{(n)}\big)_{21}-\big(\tilde{Q}_{1}^{(n)}\big)_{21}-\big(\big(Q_{1}^{(n+1)}\big)_{21}-\big(\tilde{Q}_{1}^{(n+1)}\big)_{21}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\hphantom{b_{n-1}^2 - \tilde{b}_{n-1}^2 }{}=O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{2}n}\right)\cdot O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)+\left(-\frac{1}{2i}+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{b_{n-1}^2 - \tilde{b}_{n-1}^2 =}{}\times\left(\frac{3}{16n^{2}\log^{2}n}\left(\begin{matrix}-1&i\\
i&1\end{matrix}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}\log^{3}n}\right)\right)_{21}

\displaystyle\hphantom{b_{n-1}^2 - \tilde{b}_{n-1}^2 }{}=\frac{3}{32n^{2}\log^{2}n}+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}\log^{3}n}\right).

(5.10)

Next, note that

\displaystyle b_{n-1}^{2}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}^{2}=\big(b_{n-1}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}\big)\big(b_{n-1}+\tilde{b}_{n-1}\big)=\big(b_{n-1}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}\big)\big(2\tilde{b}_{n-1}+b_{n-1}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}\big).

Recall that
\tilde{b}_{n-1}=\frac{1}{2}+O\big(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\big)
. Therefore

\displaystyle b_{n-1}^{2}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}^{2}=\big(b_{n-1}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}\big)\left(1+\big(b_{n-1}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}\big)+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)\right).

(5.11)

By definition,
b_{n-1}>0
, so
\big(1+\big(b_{n-1}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}\big)+O\big(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\big)\big)\geqslant\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon
, which combined with (
5.10
) and (
5.11
), first demonstrates

\displaystyle b_{n-1}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}\log^{2}n}\right),

which, substituted into (
5.11
) and using (
5.10
) a second time, gives

\displaystyle b_{n-1}-\tilde{b}_{n-1}=\frac{3}{32n^{2}\log^{2}n}+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}\log^{3}n}\right)

concluding the proof of Theorem
1.2
.
∎

Appendix A
Asymptotics of the Szegő function

A.1
Asymptotics of
\boldsymbol{F}

The following proves assertion (
2.12
) of Proposition
2.3

Proposition A.1
.

For
z\in\mathbb{C}_{\pm}
,

\displaystyle\frac{F^{2}}{w}(z)=\left(1\mp\frac{i\pi}{w(z)}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{2w^{2}(z)}+O\left(\frac{1}{w^{3}(z)}\right)\right)

as
z\to 1
.

Proof.

Let
z=1+re^{i\theta}
where
0<|\theta|<\pi
,
r\to 0
. We will first prove that

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}r^{-1/2}e^{-i\theta/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\log\log\frac{k}{re^{i\theta}}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\frac{k}{re^{i\theta}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{3}r}\right)\right),

where
O\big(\frac{1}{\log^{3}r}\big)
is uniform in
0<|\theta|<\pi
. Note that

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=-i\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\sqrt{1-s^{2}}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}

(A.1)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=-i\left(\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{1-s}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}+\int_{-1}^{1}\log w(s)\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-s^{2}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{1-s}}\right]\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}\right).

A calculation shows that

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\log w(s)\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-s^{2}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{1-s}}\right]\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}

(A.2)

\displaystyle=\int_{-1}^{0}\log w(s)\left[\frac{\sqrt{1-s}}{2\sqrt{1+s}+\sqrt{2}(1+s)}\right]\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}+\int_{0}^{1}\log w(s)\left[\frac{\sqrt{1-s}}{2\sqrt{1+s}+\sqrt{2}(1+s)}\right]\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}.

The first integral is clearly bounded as
z\to 1
. The second integral is easily seen to be controlled via the following lemma:

Lemma A.2
.

Let
f(x)
be an absolutely continuous function on the interval
[a,1]
with
f(1)=0
and with a derivative
f^{\prime}(x)\in L^{p}([a,1])
for some
p>1
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{1}f(s)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=O(1),\qquad\text{as $z\to+1$}

for
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash[a,1]
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{1}\frac{f(s){\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=\int_{a}^{1}\frac{f(s)-f(1){\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=-\int_{a}^{1}\int_{s}^{1}\frac{f^{\prime}(t){\rm d}t{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\int_{a}^1 \frac{f(s){\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}}{}=\int_{a}^{1}\int_{a}^{t}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{z-s}f^{\prime}(t){\rm d}t=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{a}^{1}f^{\prime}(t)\left(\log(z-t)-\log(z-a)\right){\rm d}t.

Therefore

\displaystyle\left|\int_{a}^{1}\frac{f(s){\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}\right|\leqslant\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{p}([a,1])}\times\left\|\log(z-t)-\log(z-a)\right\|_{L^{q}([a,1])}=O(1),

where
\frac{1}{q}=1-\frac{1}{p}
. Here we have used the fact that
\log(z-t)-\log(z-a)
is in
L^{q}([a,1])
uniformly as
z\to+1
,
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash[a,1]
, for all
q<\infty
.
∎

Applying Lemma
A.2
to (
A.2
) and combining with (
A.1
),

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\sqrt{1-s}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}+O(1)

as
z\to 1
uniformly for
z\in\mathbb{C}_{\pm}
. Recall
z=1+re^{i\theta}
and make the change of variables
t=1-s
, then

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\sqrt{1-s}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+re^{i\theta}}.

From Lemma
A.2
,
w(1-t)
is analytic for
s\in\mathbb{C}\backslash(-\infty,0]
and
\log w(1-t)
is analytic in the region
\mathbb{C}\backslash((-\infty,0]\cup[2k,\infty))
, so

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+re^{i\theta}}=\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\int_{C_{1}}+\int_{C_{2}}\right)\frac{\log w(1-t)}{t^{1/2}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+re^{i\theta}},

where the contours
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
are depicted in Fig.
3
for
0<\theta<\pi
; there is an analogous picture for
-\pi<\theta<0
. Here
t^{1/2}=|t|^{1/2}e^{i\theta/2}
where
-\pi<\theta<\pi
is the analytic continuation of
\sqrt{t}
from
(0,\infty)
to
\mathbb{C}\backslash(-\infty,0]
.

0
z-1=re^{i\theta}
C_{1}
C_{2}
2e^{i\theta}
2

Figure 3:
Definition of
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
.

The integral over the contour
C_{2}
is bounded away from all singularities of the integrand, and thus, by dominated convergence, tends uniformly in
0<|\theta|<\pi
as
r\to 0
to

\displaystyle\int_{C_{2}}\log w(1-t)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}},

which is finite. Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\int_{C_{1}}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{t^{1/2}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+re^{i\theta}}+O(1).

Making the change of variables
t=re^{i\theta}u
,
u>0
,
\gamma=\sqrt{u}
,

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\int_{C_{1}}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{t^{1/2}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+re^{i\theta}}=\frac{ir^{-1/2}e^{-i\theta/2}}{\sqrt{2}}\int_{0}^{2/r}\log w(1-re^{i\theta}u)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptu}{u^{3/2}+u^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\sqrt{2}ir^{-1/2}e^{-i\theta/2}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{2}r^{-1/2}}\log w\big(1-re^{i\theta}\gamma^{2}\big)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\sqrt{2}ir^{-1/2}e^{-i\theta/2}\int_{0}^{\sqrt{2}r^{-1/2}}\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}-2\log\gamma-i\theta\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\sqrt{2}ir^{-1/2}e^{-i\theta/2}\left(\int_{0}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}+\int_{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{-1/4}}+\int_{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{-1/4}}^{\sqrt{2}r^{-1/2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}-2\log\gamma-i\theta\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\sqrt{2}ir^{-1/2}e^{-i\theta/2}(N_{1}(r)+N_{2}(r)+N_{3}(r)).

(A.3)

When
\gamma\leqslant\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}
, we have that
\log\frac{2k}{r}\leqslant-4\log\gamma
. So as
-2\log\gamma>0
,

\displaystyle|N_{1}(r)|=\left|\int_{0}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}-2\log\gamma-i\theta\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}\right|

\displaystyle\hphantom{|N_1 (r) |}{}\leqslant\left|\int_{0}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}-2\log\gamma\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}\right|

\displaystyle\hphantom{|N_1 (r) |=}{}+\left|\int_{0}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}\log\left(1-\frac{i\theta}{\log\frac{2k}{r}-2\log\gamma}\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}\right|.

As
\log\frac{2k}{r}-2\log\gamma\geqslant\log\frac{2k}{r}\to\infty
as
r\to\infty
, the integrand in the second integral is bounded above and therefore

\displaystyle|N_{1}(r)|\leqslant\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}\left|\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}-2\log\gamma\right)\right|\frac{{\rm d}\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}+O\big(r^{1/4}\big).

|\log x|
is an increasing function when
x>1
, therefore since
\log\frac{2k}{r}\leqslant-4\log\gamma
and
\log\frac{2k}{r}-2\log\gamma>1
as
r\to 0
,

\displaystyle|N_{1}(r)|\leqslant\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}\log\left(-6\log\gamma\right)\frac{{\rm d}\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}+O\big(r^{1/4}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{|N_1(r)|}{}\leqslant\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}\log\left(-6\log\gamma\right){\rm d}\gamma+O\big(r^{1/4}\big)

Integrating by parts,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}\log(-6\log\gamma){\rm d}\gamma=\log(-6\log\gamma)\gamma\bigg|_{0}^{r^{1/4}/(2k)^{1/4}}-\int_{0}^{r^{1/4}/(2k)^{1/4}}\frac{1}{\log\gamma}{\rm d}\gamma

\displaystyle\hphantom{\int_0^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}} \log(- 6\log\gamma) {\rm d}\gamma}{}=O\big(r^{1/4}\log(-\log r)\big),

and so

\displaystyle|N_{1}(r)|=O\big(r^{1/4}\log(-\log r)\big).

(A.4)

Now,

\displaystyle|N_{3}(r)|=\left|\int_{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{-1/4}}^{\sqrt{2}r^{-1/2}}\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}-2\log\gamma-i\theta\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}\right|,

which, after the change of variables
y=\frac{1}{\gamma}
, becomes

\displaystyle\left|\int^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}_{r^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}}\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}+2\log y-i\theta\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pty}{y^{2}+1}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad{}\leqslant\frac{1}{2\pi}\int^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}_{r^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}}\left|\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}+2\log y-i\theta\right)\right|\frac{{\rm d}y}{y^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad{}\leqslant\frac{1}{2\pi}\int^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}_{r^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}}\left|\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}+2\log y\right)^{2}+\theta^{2}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\quad{}+i\arg\left(\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}+2\log y-i\theta\right)\right)\right|\frac{{\rm d}y}{y^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad{}\leqslant\frac{1}{4\pi}\int^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}_{r^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}}\left|\log\left(\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}+2\log y\right)^{2}+\theta^{2}\right)\right|\frac{{\rm d}y}{y^{2}+1}+O\big(r^{1/4}\big).

When
\frac{r^{1/2}}{\sqrt{2}}\leqslant y\leqslant\big(\frac{r}{2k}\big)^{1/4}
, we have that
-2\log y+\log\frac{2k}{2}\leqslant\log\frac{2k}{r}\leqslant-4\log y
. So

\displaystyle|N_{3}(r)|\leqslant\frac{1}{4\pi}\int^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}_{r^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}}\left(\big|\log\big((-2\log y)^{2}+\theta^{2}\big)\big|+\left|\log\left(\left(\log\frac{2k}{2}\right)^{2}+\theta^{2}\right)\right|\right)\frac{{\rm d}y}{y^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad{}\leqslant\frac{1}{4\pi}\int^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}_{r^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}}\left|\log\left(4\log^{2}y+\theta^{2}\right)\right|\frac{{\rm d}y}{y^{2}+1}+O\big(r^{1/4}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}\leqslant\frac{1}{4\pi}\int^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}_{r^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}}\left|\log\left(4\log^{2}y\right)\right|\frac{{\rm d}y}{y^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}_{r^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}}\left|\log\left(1+\frac{\theta^{2}}{4\log^{2}y}\right)\right|\frac{{\rm d}y}{y^{2}+1}+O\big(r^{1/4}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}\leqslant\frac{1}{2\pi}\int^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}_{r^{1/2}/\sqrt{2}}\log\left(-2\log y\right)\frac{{\rm d}y}{y^{2}+1}+O\big(r^{1/4}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=O\big(r^{1/4}\log(-\log r)\big),

(A.5)

where, in the final step, we use the same argument as in the proof of (
A.4
).

When
\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}\leqslant\gamma\leqslant\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{-1/4}
, we have
|\log\gamma|\leqslant\frac{1}{4}\left|\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}\right|
. Therefore for
\gamma
in this region, we can use the power series expansion for the logarithm

\displaystyle\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{r}-2\log\gamma-i\theta\right)=\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}-2\log\gamma\right)=\log\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}+\log\left(1-\frac{2\log\gamma}{\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\log\left(\log\frac{2k}{r} - 2\log\gamma- i\theta\right)}{}=\log\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}-\frac{2\log\gamma}{\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}-\frac{2\log^{2}\gamma}{\log^{2}\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}+O\left(\frac{\log^{3}\gamma}{\log^{3}r}\right),

where again the term
O\left(\frac{\log^{3}\gamma}{\log^{3}r}\right)
is uniform in
\theta
. Therefore

\displaystyle N_{2}(r)=\int_{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{-1/4}}\left[\log\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}-\frac{2\log\gamma}{\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}-\frac{2\log^{2}\gamma}{\log^{2}\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}+O\left(\frac{\log^{3}\gamma}{\log^{3}r}\right)\right]\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\log\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}\int_{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{-1/4}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}-\frac{1}{\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}\int_{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{-1/4}}\frac{2\log\gamma{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}-\frac{1}{\log^{2}\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}\int_{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{-1/4}}\frac{2\log^{2}\gamma{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}+\frac{1}{\log^{3}r}\int_{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{1/4}}^{\left(\frac{r}{2k}\right)^{-1/4}}\frac{O\left(\log^{3}\gamma\right)}{\gamma^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\log\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}-\frac{1}{\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{2\log\gamma{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}-\frac{1}{\log^{2}\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{2\log^{2}\gamma{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{3}r}\right).

(A.6)

The first integral in (
A.6
) is easily calculated

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\arctan\gamma\bigg|_{0}^{\infty}=\frac{1}{4i}.

(A.7)

The second integral, after a change of variables
\eta=\frac{1}{\gamma}
, satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\log\gamma{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\log\eta{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\eta}{\eta^{2}+1},

and so

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\log\gamma{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}=0.

(A.8)

The final integral can be calculated by the method of residues, which yields:

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\log^{2}\gamma{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}=\frac{\pi^{2}}{8i}.

(A.9)

Combining (
A.6
) with (
A.7
), (
A.8
), and (
A.9
), we see that

\displaystyle N_{2}(r)=\frac{1}{4i}\log\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{8i}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{3}r}\right),

(A.10)

and combining (
A.3
) with (
A.4
), (
A.5
), and (
A.10
), we see that

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}r^{-1/2}e^{-i\theta/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\log\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\frac{2k}{r}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{3}r}\right)\right)+O(1).

(A.11)

Now note that for
z=1+re^{i\theta}
,

\displaystyle\big(z^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}=\sqrt{2}r^{1/2}e^{i\theta/2}+O(r).

(A.12)

So combining (
A.11
) and (
A.12
) with the definition of
F
, we see that

\displaystyle\log F(z)=\frac{1}{2}\log\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{3}r}\right),

and so

\displaystyle F^{2}(z)=\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}\left(1-\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}\frac{1}{\log^{2}\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\log^{3}r}\right)\right).

Now for
z=1+re^{i\theta}\in\mathbb{C}_{\pm}
, and recalling that
\log
always refers to the principle branch,

\displaystyle\log\frac{2k}{re^{i\theta}}=\log\frac{2k}{re^{i(\theta\mp\pi)}}\mp i\pi=\log\frac{2k}{-re^{i\theta}}\mp i\pi=w(z)\mp i\pi

from which it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{F^{2}}{w}(z)=1\mp\frac{i\pi}{w(z)}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{2w^{2}(z)}+O\left(\frac{1}{w^{3}(z)}\right)

uniformly for
z\in\mathbb{C}_{\pm}
as
z\to 1
, which completes the proof of Proposition
A.1
.
∎

The following proves assertion (
2.13
) of Proposition
2.3

Proposition A.3
.

\displaystyle\frac{F^{2}}{w}(z)=1+O\big(|z+1|^{1/2}\big)

as
z\to-1
,
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash[-1,1]
.

Proof.

Note that as
z\to-1
, uniformly for
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash[-1,1]
,

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(-1)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}+\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)-\log(w(-1))}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}.

By the proof of Lemma
A.2
, the second integral is easily seen to be
O(1)
. Therefore

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(-1)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}+O(1)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\int_{-1}^1 \frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}} \frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}}{}=\frac{1}{2}\log w(-1)\int_{C}\frac{1}{(s^{2}-1)^{1/2}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}+O(1),

where
C
refers to any clockwise contour containing
[-1,1]
but not the point
z
. Letting
C
go to infinity, a residue calculation gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\log w(-1)\int_{C}\frac{1}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log w(-1)}{\big(z^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}}.

Thus

\displaystyle F^{2}(z)=\exp\left(2\big(z^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-z}\right)=\exp\big(\log w(-1)+O\big(|z+1|^{1/2}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{F^2(z)}{}=w(-1)+O\big(|z+1|^{1/2}\big),

and so

\displaystyle\frac{F^{2}}{w}(z)=1+O\big(|z+1|^{1/2}\big)

as
z\to-1
,
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash[-1,1]
, which proves Proposition
A.3
.
∎

A.2
Estimate on
\boldsymbol{\frac{F^{2}}{w}(1+r)-\frac{F^{2}}{w}(1+\tilde{r})}
,
\boldsymbol{r,\tilde{r}>0}

Proposition A.4
.

Fix
R>0
and suppose
r,\tilde{r}>0
obey

\displaystyle r,\tilde{r}=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right),\qquad n\left(\frac{r}{\tilde{r}}-1\right)\in[-R,R].

Then

\displaystyle\frac{F^{2}}{w_{+}}(1+r)-\frac{F^{2}}{w_{+}}(1+\tilde{r})+\frac{F^{2}}{w_{-}}(1+r)-\frac{F^{2}}{w_{-}}(1+\tilde{r})=O_{R}\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right).

Remark A.5
.

Notice that if
r=O\big(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\big)
, then Proposition
2.4
implies
\frac{F^{2}}{w_{+}}(1+r)+\frac{F^{2}}{w_{-}}(1+r)-2=O\big(\frac{1}{\log^{2}n}\big)
. Also notice that
\frac{1}{\log^{2}n}-\frac{1}{\log^{2}(n+c)}=O\big(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\big)
. Therefore we would expect
\frac{F^{2}}{w_{+}}(1+r)-\frac{F^{2}}{w_{+}}(1+\tilde{r})+\frac{F^{2}}{w_{-}}(1+r)-\frac{F^{2}}{w_{-}}(1+\tilde{r})=O\big(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\big)
for sufficiently close
r
,
\tilde{r}
. Proposition
A.4
makes this rigorous.

Proof.

We define
a=a(r,\tilde{r}):=n\big(\frac{r}{\tilde{r}}-1\big)
. Clearly
\frac{r}{\tilde{r}}=1+\frac{a}{n}
. In the calculations that follow, we assume without loss of generality that
r>\tilde{r}
, and hence
a>0
. First, note that

\displaystyle\log F(1+r)=\big((1+r)^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+r)}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\log F(1+r)}{}=\big(\sqrt{2}r^{1/2}+O\big(r^{3/2}\big)\big)\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+r)}.

(A.13)

By (
A.11
), which holds uniformly for
z\in\mathbb{C}_{\pm}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{(s^{2}-1)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+r)}=O\big(r^{-1/2}\log\log r\big),

and so, since
r=O\big(n^{-2}\big)
,

\displaystyle\log F(1+r)=\sqrt{2}r^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{(s^{2}-1)^{1/2}_{+}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+r)}+O\left(\frac{\log\log n}{n^{2}}\right).

Therefore

\displaystyle\log\frac{F(1+r)}{F(1+\tilde{r})}=\sqrt{2}r^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)_{+}^{1/2}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+r)}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\log\frac{F(1 + r)}{F(1 + \tilde{r})} =}{}-\sqrt{2}\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)_{+}^{1/2}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+\tilde{r})}+O\left(\frac{\log\log n}{n^{2}}\right).

(A.14)

In (
A.14
),

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}r^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)_{+}^{1/2}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+r)}-\sqrt{2}\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\big(s^{2}-1\big)_{+}^{1/2}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+\tilde{r})}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=-\sqrt{2}ir^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\sqrt{1-s^{2}}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+r)}+\sqrt{2}i\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\sqrt{1-s^{2}}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+\tilde{r})}.

Making the change of variables
t=1-s
,

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}ir^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\sqrt{1-s^{2}}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+r)}+\sqrt{2}i\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\log w(s)}{\sqrt{1-s^{2}}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts}{s-(1+\tilde{r})}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=-\sqrt{2}ir^{1/2}\int_{2}^{0}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t(2-t)}}\frac{-{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{-t-r}+\sqrt{2}i\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{2}^{0}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t(2-t)}}\frac{-{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{-t-\tilde{r}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\sqrt{2}ir^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t(2-t)}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+r}-\sqrt{2}i\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t(2-t)}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+\tilde{r}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=ir^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+r}-i\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+\tilde{r}}+iH(r,\tilde{r}),

(A.15)

where

\displaystyle H(r,\tilde{r}):=\sqrt{2}\left[r^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t(2-t)}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+r}-\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t(2-t)}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+\tilde{r}}\right]

\displaystyle\hphantom{H(r,\tilde{r}) :=}{}-\left[r^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+r}-\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+\tilde{r}}\right].

The same calculations as in the proof of Proposition
A.1
show that

\displaystyle H(r,\tilde{r})=r^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\log w(1-t)\left[\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2-t}+(2-t)}\right]\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+r}

\displaystyle\hphantom{H(r,\tilde{r})=}{}-\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\log w(1-t)\left[\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2-t}+(2-t)}\right]\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+\tilde{r}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{H(r,\tilde{r})}{}=\big(r^{1/2}-\tilde{r}^{1/2}\big)\int_{0}^{2}\log w(1-t)\left[\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2-t}+(2-t)}\right]\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+r}

\displaystyle\hphantom{H(r,\tilde{r})=}{}+\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\log w(1-t)\left[\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2-t}+(2-t)}\right]\left(\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+r}-\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+\tilde{r}}\right).

So

\displaystyle|H(r,\tilde{r})|\leqslant\big|r^{1/2}-\tilde{r}^{1/2}\big|\int_{0}^{2}|\log w(1-t)|\left[\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2-t}+(2-t)}\right]\frac{{\rm d}t}{t}

\displaystyle\hphantom{|H(r, \tilde{r})|\leqslant}{}+\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}|\log w(1-t)|\left[\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2-t}+(2-t)}\right]\frac{|\tilde{r}-r|{\rm d}t}{|t+r|\cdot|t+\tilde{r}|}

\displaystyle\hphantom{|H(r, \tilde{r})|}{}\leqslant c_{1}\tilde{r}^{1/2}\left|\frac{r^{1/2}}{\tilde{r}^{1/2}}-1\right|+\tilde{r}^{1/2}\frac{|\tilde{r}-r|}{\tilde{r}}\int_{0}^{2}|\log w(1-t)|\left[\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2-t}+(2-t)}\right]\frac{{\rm d}t}{t}

\displaystyle\hphantom{|H(r, \tilde{r})|}{}\leqslant c_{1}\tilde{r}^{1/2}\left|\frac{r^{1/2}}{\tilde{r}^{1/2}}-1\right|+c_{2}\tilde{r}^{1/2}\left|\frac{r}{\tilde{r}}-1\right|=O\big(n^{-2}\big).

Making the changes of variables
t\to rt
and
t\to\tilde{r}t
in (
A.15
) within the respective integrals, we have

\displaystyle ir^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+r}-i\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\log w(1-t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+\tilde{r}}

\displaystyle\quad{}=ir^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2/r}\frac{\log w(1-rt)}{\sqrt{rt}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+1}-i\tilde{r}^{1/2}\int_{0}^{2/\tilde{r}}\frac{\log w(1-\tilde{r}t)}{\sqrt{\tilde{r}t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+1}

\displaystyle\quad{}=i\int_{0}^{2/r}\frac{\log w(1-rt)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+1}-i\int_{0}^{2/r}\frac{\log w(1-\tilde{r}t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+1}-i\int_{2/r}^{2/\tilde{r}}\frac{\log w(1-\tilde{r}t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+1}

\displaystyle\quad{}=i\int_{0}^{2/r}\left(\log w(1-rt)-\log w(1-\tilde{r}t)\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}-i\int_{2/r}^{2/\tilde{r}}\frac{\log w(1-\tilde{r}t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+1}.

(A.16)

Note that, as
\log k\leqslant\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\leqslant\log\frac{k}{1+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)}
for
t\in[\frac{2}{r},\frac{2}{\tilde{r}}]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{2/r}^{2/\tilde{r}}\frac{\log w(1-\tilde{r}t)}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t+1}\right|\leqslant\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{1}{t+1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(2/r,2/\tilde{r})}\int_{2/r}^{2/\tilde{r}}\left|\log w(1-\tilde{r}t)\right|{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt

\displaystyle\hphantom{\left|\int_{2/r}^{2/\tilde{r}} \frac{\log w(1-\tilde{r}t)}{\sqrt{t}} \frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t +1}\right|}{}\leqslant cr^{3/2}\left|\frac{2}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{2}{r}\right|=cr^{1/2}\left|\frac{r}{\tilde{r}}-1\right|=O\big(n^{-2}\big).

To estimate the remaining term in (
A.16
),

\displaystyle i\int_{0}^{2/r}\left(\log w(1-rt)-\log w(1-\tilde{r}t)\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=i\int_{0}^{2/r}\log\left(\frac{w(1-rt)}{w(1-\tilde{r}t)}\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}.

(A.17)

Now, for
t\in[0,\frac{2}{r}]
,

\displaystyle\frac{w(1-rt)}{w(1-\tilde{r}t)}=\frac{\log\big(\frac{2k}{rt}\big)}{\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}=1+\frac{\log\big(\frac{2k}{rt}\big)-\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}{\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\frac{w(1 - rt)}{w(1-\tilde{r}t)}}{}=1+\frac{\log\frac{\tilde{r}}{r}}{\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}=1+\frac{\log\big(1+\frac{a}{n}\big)}{\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}=1+\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}+O\big(n^{-2}\big).

So

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{w(1-rt)}{w(1-\tilde{r}t)}\right)=\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}+O\big(n^{-2}\big).

(A.18)

Substituting (
A.18
) into (
A.17
) yields

\displaystyle i\int_{0}^{2/r}\left(\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}+O\big(n^{-2}\big)\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=i\int_{r^{1/2}}^{r^{-1/2}}\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}+E(r)+O\big(n^{-2}\big),

where

\displaystyle E(r)=i\int_{0}^{r^{1/2}}\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}+i\int_{r^{-1/2}}^{2/r}\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}.

Now

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{r^{1/2}}\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}\right|\leqslant\int_{0}^{r^{1/2}}\frac{c}{n|\log r|}\frac{dt}{t^{1/2}}\leqslant\frac{cr^{1/4}}{n\log r}=O\big(n^{-3/2}\big).

Similarly

\displaystyle\left|\int_{r^{-1/2}}^{2/r}\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}\right|\leqslant\int_{r^{-1/2}}^{2/r}\frac{c}{n|\log r|}\frac{dt}{t^{3/2}}\leqslant\frac{cr^{1/4}}{n\log r}=O\big(n^{-3/2}\big).

Therefore,

\displaystyle|E(r)|=O\big(n^{-3/2}\big),

and so

\displaystyle i\int_{0}^{2/r}\left(\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}+O\big(n^{-2}\big)\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=i\int_{r^{1/2}}^{r^{-1/2}}\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}t}\big)}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}+O\big(n^{-3/2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=i\int_{r^{1/2}}^{r^{-1/2}}\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)-n\log t}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}+O\big(n^{-3/2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=i\int_{r^{1/2}}^{r^{-1/2}}\frac{a}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}\frac{1}{1-\frac{\log t}{\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}+O\big(n^{-3/2}\big),

(A.19)

but
t\in[r^{1/2},r^{-1/2}]
and so
|\log t|\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{1}{r}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{2k}{r}
. Therefore, (
A.19
) is

\displaystyle\frac{ia}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}\int_{r^{1/2}}^{r^{-1/2}}\left(1+\frac{\log t}{\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}+O\left(\frac{\log^{2}t}{\log^{2}\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}\right)\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}+O\big(n^{-3/2}\big).

Making the change of variables
\gamma=t^{1/2}
, this becomes

\displaystyle\frac{2ia}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}\int_{r^{1/4}}^{r^{-1/4}}\left(1+\frac{2\log\gamma}{\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}+O\left(\frac{\log^{2}\gamma}{\log^{2}\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}\right)\right)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}+O\big(n^{-3/2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{2ia}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}\left(\int_{r^{1/4}}^{r^{-1/4}}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}+\int_{r^{1/4}}^{r^{-1/4}}\frac{2\log\gamma}{\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{2ia}{n\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{2\log\gamma}{\log\big(\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big)}\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma}{\gamma^{2}+1}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right).

So, using the same calculations of the above integrals, see (
A.7
) and (
A.8
), as in the proof of Proposition
A.1
,

\displaystyle i\int_{0}^{2/r}\big(\log w(1-rt)-\log w(1-\tilde{r}t)\big)\frac{{\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006ptt}{t^{3/2}+t^{1/2}}=\frac{a}{2n\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right).

(A.20)

So, combining (
A.13
), (
A.15
), (
A.16
), and (
A.20
), we obtain

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{F^{2}(1+r)}{F^{2}(1+\tilde{r})}\right)=2\log\frac{F(1+r)}{F(1+\tilde{r})}=\frac{a}{n\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right),

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{F^{2}(1+r)}{F^{2}(1+\tilde{r})}=\exp\left(\frac{a}{n\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right)\right)=1+\frac{a}{n\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right).

Now,

\displaystyle\frac{F^{2}}{w_{\pm}}(1+r)-\frac{F^{2}}{w_{\pm}}(1+\tilde{r})=\frac{F^{2}(1+r)-F^{2}(1+\tilde{r})}{w_{\pm}(1+r)}+F^{2}(1+\tilde{r})\left(\frac{1}{w_{\pm}(1+r)}-\frac{1}{w_{\pm}(1+\tilde{r})}\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\frac{F^{2}}{w_{\pm}}(1 + r) - \frac{F^{2}}{w_{\pm}} (1 + \tilde{r}) }{}=\frac{F^{2}(1+\tilde{r})}{w_{\pm}(1+r)}\left(\frac{F^{2}(1+r)}{F^{2}(1+\tilde{r})}-1\right)+\frac{F^{2}(1+\tilde{r})}{w_{\pm}(1+r)}\left(1-\frac{w_{\pm}(1+r)}{w_{\pm}(1+\tilde{r})}\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\frac{F^{2}}{w_{\pm}}(1 + r) - \frac{F^{2}}{w_{\pm}} (1 + \tilde{r}) }{}=\frac{F^{2}(1+\tilde{r})}{w_{\pm}(1+r)}\left(\frac{a}{n\log\frac{2k}{r}}+O\!\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right)+1-\frac{w_{\pm}(1+r)}{w_{\pm}(1+\tilde{r})}\right)\!,\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

(A.21)

where

\displaystyle\frac{w_{\pm}(1+r)}{w_{\pm}(1+\tilde{r})}=\frac{\log\frac{2k}{r}\pm i\pi}{\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\pm i\pi}=1+\frac{\log\frac{2k}{r}-\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}}{\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\pm i\pi}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\frac{w_{\pm}(1 +r)}{w_{\pm}(1 + \tilde{r})}}{}=1+\frac{\log\frac{\tilde{r}}{r}}{\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\pm i\pi}=1+\frac{a}{n\left(\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\pm i\pi\right)}+O\big(n^{-2}\big),

so

\displaystyle\frac{a}{n\log\frac{2k}{r}}+1-\frac{w_{\pm}(1+r)}{w_{\pm}(1+\tilde{r})}=\frac{a}{n\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}}-\frac{a}{n\big(\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\pm i\pi\big)}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\frac{a}{n\log\frac{2k}{r}} + 1 - \frac{w_{\pm}(1 +r)}{w_{\pm}(1 + \tilde{r})}}{}=\frac{\pm i\pi a}{n\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\big(\log\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}\pm i\pi\big)}=\pm\frac{i\pi a}{n\log^{2}\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right).

(A.22)

Substituting (
A.22
) into (
A.21
),

\displaystyle\frac{F^{2}}{w_{\pm}}(1+r)-\frac{F^{2}}{w_{\pm}}(1+\tilde{r})=\frac{F^{2}(1+\tilde{r})}{w_{\pm}(1+r)}\left(\pm\frac{i\pi a}{n\log^{2}\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\frac{F^{2}}{w_{\pm}}(1 + r) - \frac{F^{2}}{w_{\pm}} (1 + \tilde{r})}{}=\left(\pm\frac{i\pi a}{n\log^{2}\frac{2k}{\tilde{r}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right)\right)\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\log n}\right)\right),

where we have used Proposition
A.1
. So, in particular

\displaystyle\frac{F^{2}}{w_{+}}(1+r)-\frac{F^{2}}{w_{+}}(1+\tilde{r})+\frac{F^{2}}{w_{-}}(1+r)-\frac{F^{2}}{w_{-}}(1+\tilde{r})=O\left(\frac{1}{n\log^{3}n}\right),

which concludes the proof of Proposition
2.5
.
∎

Appendix B
A summary of Legendre asymptotics

In this appendix we prove a series of
L^{2}
estimates, ultimately allowing us to replace the integral in Proposition
4.9
with an integral only involving Legendre quantities, as in Proposition
5.2
.

B.1
A summary of Legendre asymptotics

In
[
13
]
the authors derive the asymptotics of the solution
S(z)=S^{(n)}(z)
to the RHP
(\Sigma_{S},v_{S})
:

(a)

\displaystyle S(z)\text{ is analytic for $z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\Sigma_{S}$},

(B.1)

(b)

S
satisfies the following jump relation on
s\in\Sigma_{S}

\displaystyle S_{+}(s)=S_{-}(s)v_{S}(s),

(B.2)

(c)

S(z)
has the following behavior at infinity

\displaystyle S(z)=\left(I+O\left(\frac{1}{z}\right)\right),\qquad\text{as $z\to\infty$},

(B.3)

(d)

S(z)
has the following behavior as
z\to 1

\displaystyle S(z)=O\left(\begin{matrix}\log|z-1|&\log|z-1|\\
\log|z-1|&\log|z-1|\end{matrix}\right),

(B.4)

(e)

S(z)
has the following behavior as
z\to-1

\displaystyle S(z)=O\left(\begin{matrix}\log|z+1|&\log|z+1|\\
\log|z+1|&\log|z+1|\end{matrix}\right),

(B.5)

where
\Sigma_{S}
is depicted in Fig.
5
,

\displaystyle v_{S}(s)=\begin{cases}\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
\phi(s)^{-2n}&1\end{matrix}\right)&\text{for $s$ in the upper and lower lips},\\
\left(\begin{matrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{matrix}\right)&\text{for $s\in[-1,1]$}.\end{cases}

The asymptotics are derived for
z
in the regions
A
,
B
,
C
,
D
, and
E
, see Fig.
6
. We will summarize the necessary results in this section.

Recall that
\tilde{Q}
is the solution to (
3.12
)–(
3.14
)
(\Sigma,\tilde{v}_{\Sigma})
.
S
is the solution to (
B.1
)–(
B.5
)
(\Sigma_{S},v_{S}
). See Figs.
4
and
5
for depictions of the contours
\Sigma
and
\Sigma_{S}
. The RHP for
S
differs from the RHP for
\tilde{Q}
near the point 1, but the problems are related via the deformation:

\displaystyle\tilde{Q}(z)=\begin{cases}S(z)\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
\phi^{-2n}(z)&1\end{matrix}\right)&\text{for $z\in\Omega^{\prime}_{1}$},\\
S(z)\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
-\phi^{-2n}(z)&1\end{matrix}\right)&\text{for $z\in\Omega^{\prime}_{2}$},\\
S(z)&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(B.6)

where the regions

For

, we define, as in
[

]
,

\displaystyle N(z)=\left(\begin{matrix}\dfrac{a+a^{-1}}{2}&\dfrac{a-a^{-1}}{2i}\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\dfrac{a-a^{-1}}{-2i}&\dfrac{a+a^{-1}}{2}\end{matrix}\right),

where

\displaystyle a:=a(z)=\left(\frac{z-1}{z+1}\right)^{1/4},

where
a(z)
is analytic for
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash[-1,1]
and
a(z)>0
when

. Let
U_{\delta}(s)
refer to the closed ball of radius
\delta
centered as
s=\pm 1
. The regions
B
and
C
are given by
B=U_{\delta}(1)\backslash\Sigma_{S}
and
C=U_{\delta}(-1)\backslash\Sigma_{S}
respectively. We define

\displaystyle f(z)=\frac{\log^{2}\phi(z)}{4}.

f(z)
is analytic in a neighborhood of
1
and

\displaystyle f(1)=0,

\displaystyle f(z)=\frac{1}{2}(z-1)+O\big(|z-1|^{2}\big)\qquad\text{as $z\to 1$},

\displaystyle f(-z)=-\frac{1}{2}(z+1)+O\big(|z+1|^{2}\big)\qquad\text{as $z\to-1$}.

(B.7)

So, in particular, we can pick
\delta
sufficiently small so that
f(z)
is locally conformal for
z\in B
, which clearly also implies
f(-z)
will be locally conformal in
C
. We define

\displaystyle\Psi(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\left(\begin{matrix}I_{0}\big(2\zeta^{1/2}\big)&\frac{i}{\pi}K_{0}\big(2\zeta^{1/2}\big)\\
2\pi i\zeta^{1/2}I_{0}^{\prime}\big(2\zeta^{1/2}\big)&-2\zeta^{1/2}K_{0}^{\prime}\big(2\zeta^{1/2}\big)\end{matrix}\right)\\
\hskip 227.62204pt\text{for $|\arg(\zeta)|<2\pi/3$},\\
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{2}H_{0}^{(1)}\big(2(-\zeta)^{1/2}\big)&\frac{1}{2}H_{0}^{(2)}(2(-\zeta^{1/2}))\\
\pi\zeta^{1/2}\big(H_{0}^{(1)}\big)^{\prime}\big(2(-\zeta)^{1/2}\big)&\pi\zeta^{1/2}\big(H_{0}^{(2)}\big)^{\prime}\big(2(-\zeta)^{1/2}\big)\end{matrix}\right)\\
\hskip 227.62204pt\text{for $2\pi/3<\arg\zeta<\pi$},\\
\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{2}H_{0}^{(2)}\big(2(-\zeta)^{1/2}\big)&-\frac{1}{2}H_{0}^{(1)}(2(-\zeta^{1/2}))\\
-\pi\zeta^{1/2}\big(H_{0}^{(2)}\big)^{\prime}\big(2(-\zeta)^{1/2}\big)&\pi\zeta^{1/2}\big(H_{0}^{(1)}\big)^{\prime}\big(2(-\zeta)^{1/2}\big)\end{matrix}\right)\\
\hskip 227.62204pt\text{for $-\pi<\arg\zeta<-2\pi/3$},\end{cases}\!\!\!\!\!

(B.8)

for
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\Gamma
, and where
\Gamma
is defined in Fig.
8
. The contour
\Sigma_{S}
is chosen within
U_{\delta}(1)
, (resp.
-1
) to be the preimage of
\Gamma
under the map

(resp.
n^{2}f(-z)
). Therefore, around the point
-1
,
\Sigma
is the preimage of
\Gamma
under the map

as well. In other words

\displaystyle n^{2}f(-(\Sigma\cap U_{\delta}(-1)))\subset\Gamma\qquad\text{for all $n$}.

(B.9)

In (
B.8
),
I_{0}
,
K_{0}
refer to the modified Bessel functions of the first and second kinds with parameter
0
, and
H^{(1)}_{0}
,

refer to the Hankel functions of the first and second kinds, see, for instance,
[
1
]
. As before, the square root in the above definition is given by its principle value.

For any fixed

sufficiently small, the following holds uniformly,

\displaystyle S^{(n)}(z)=\begin{cases}R^{(n)}(z)N(z)&\text{for $z\in A\cup D\cup E$},\\

\sigma_{3}R^{(n)}(-z)E(-z)(2\pi n)^{\sigma_{3}/2}\Psi\big(n^{2}f(-z)\big)\phi^{-n\sigma_{3}}(-z)\sigma_{3}&\text{for $z\in C$},\end{cases}\hskip-28.45274pt

(B.10)

where
R^{(n)}(z)
is bounded, analytic in

, with
\Sigma_{R}
depicted in Fig.

, and

\displaystyle R^{(n)}(z)=I+\frac{R_{1}(z)}{n}+O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)\qquad\text{uniformly in $z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\Sigma_{R}$},

where
R_{1}(z)
is an
n
-independent, bounded, analytic function for
z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\Sigma_{R}
. Additionally

\displaystyle\left|R^{(n)}(z)-I-\frac{R_{1}(z)}{n}\right|\leqslant\frac{1}{n^{2}|z|}.

(B.11)

is defined by (see Remark

)

\displaystyle E(z)=N(z)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{matrix}1&-i\\

E(z)
is analytic and bounded for

and has determinant 1. It follows, in particular, that

is also analytic and bounded for

.

Remark B.1
.

We use slightly different notation than in
[
13
]
in a few ways,

•

We refer to
R(z)
as
R^{(n)}(z)
to emphasize its dependence on
n
.

•

The neighborhoods
U_{\delta}
and
\tilde{U}_{\delta}
we refer to as
U_{\delta}(1)
and
U_{\delta}(-1)
respectively.

•

The matching factor
E_{n}(z)
in
[
13
]
we write
E_{n}(z)=E(z)(2\pi n)^{\sigma_{3}/2}
, where
E(z)
is clearly independent of
n
.

In estimating the integral

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\mu(s)(v_{\Sigma}(s)-\tilde{v}_{\Sigma}(s))\tilde{\mu}^{-1}(s){\rm d}\hskip-1.00006pt\bar{}\hskip 1.00006pts

we need asymptotic information about the function
\tilde{\mu}
. However, since
v_{\Sigma}(s)-\tilde{v}_{\Sigma}(s)
is identically 0 for
s\in(-1,1)
and is strictly lower triangular otherwise, we actually only need information about
\tilde{\mu}
’s second column. To that end, let
\tilde{\mu}_{2},\tilde{Q}_{2}
, and
S_{2}
refer to the second columns of the matrices
\tilde{\mu}
,
\tilde{Q}
, and
S
.

It is clear from the RHP for
\tilde{Q}
, (
3.12
)–(
3.14
), that
\tilde{Q}_{2}
is actually analytic in
\mathbb{C}\backslash[-1,1]
, and it is clear from the deformation (
B.6
) that
\tilde{Q}_{2}=S_{2}
. Similarly,
S_{2}
is analytic in
\mathbb{C}\backslash[-1,1]
. Therefore, for
z\in\Sigma\backslash[-1,1]
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{2}(z)=\big(\tilde{Q}_{2}(z)\big)_{-}=\tilde{Q}_{2}(z)=S_{2}(z).

For
z\in(\Sigma\cap\Sigma_{2})\backslash[-1,1]
,
\tilde{\mu}_{2}(z)=S_{+}(z)=S_{-}(z)
and hence, in evaluating
\tilde{\mu}_{2}(z)
we can, when convenient, use either applicable expression from (
B.8
) or (
B.10
).

We will always pick the “outside” interpretation for
S_{2}(z)
in such cases. When
z\in\Sigma\backslash(\Sigma_{S}\cap\Sigma)
(see again Fig.
7
), we are outside of the contour
\Sigma_{S}
. It follows from (
B.10
), uniformly as
n\to\infty
for
z\in\Sigma\backslash[-1,1]
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}_{2}(z)=\begin{cases}R^{(n)}(z)\left(\begin{matrix}\dfrac{a-a^{-1}}{2i}\\
\dfrac{a+a^{-1}}{2}\end{matrix}\right)\\
\hskip 142.26378pt\text{for $z\in\Sigma\backslash([-1,1]\cup U_{\delta}(1)\cup U_{\delta}(-1))$},\\
R^{(n)}(z)E(z)(2\pi n)^{\sigma_{3}/2}\Psi_{2}\big(n^{2}f(z)\big)\phi^{n}(z)\\
\hskip 142.26378pt\text{for $z\in(\Sigma\backslash[-1,1])\cap U_{\delta}(1)$},\\
-\sigma_{3}R^{(n)}(-z)E(-z)(2\pi n)^{\sigma_{3}/2}\Psi_{2}\big(n^{2}f(-z)\big)\phi^{n}(-z)\\
\hskip 142.26378pt\text{for $z\in(\Sigma\backslash[-1,1])\cap U_{\delta}(-1)$},\end{cases}

(B.12)

where

\displaystyle\Psi_{2}(\zeta)=\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{i}{\pi}K_{0}\big(2\zeta^{1/2}\big)\\
-2\zeta^{1/2}K_{0}^{\prime}\big(2\zeta^{1/2}\big)\end{matrix}\right).

Note, from the properties of the modified Bessel function
K_{0}
, that
\Psi_{2}(\zeta)
has an analytic extension to
\mathbb{C}\backslash(-\infty,0]
. From (
B.10
) and (
B.12
), we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}(z)=\begin{cases}R^{(n)}(z)E(z)(2\pi n)^{\sigma_{3}/2}\Psi\big(n^{2}f(z)\big)\phi^{-n\sigma_{3}}(z)\\
\hskip 142.26378pt\text{for $(\Sigma\backslash[-1,1])\cap U_{\delta}(1)$},\\
-\sigma_{3}R^{(n)}(-z)E(-z)(2\pi n)^{\sigma_{3}/2}\Psi\big(n^{2}f(-z)\big)\phi^{-n\sigma_{3}}(-z)\sigma_{3}\\
\hskip 142.26378pt\text{for $z\in(\Sigma\backslash[-1,1])\cap U_{\delta}(-1)$},\end{cases}

(B.13)

where

\displaystyle\Psi=\left(\begin{matrix}\Psi_{11}&\Psi_{12}\\
\Psi_{21}&\Psi_{22}\end{matrix}\right),\qquad\Psi_{12}(\zeta)=\frac{i}{\pi}K_{0}\big(2\zeta^{1/2}\big),\qquad\Psi_{22}(\zeta)=-2\zeta^{1/2}K_{0}^{\prime}\big(2\zeta^{1/2}\big).

We will repeatedly make use of the following asymptotic estimates for the functions
\Psi_{12}
,
\Psi_{22}
:

\displaystyle\Psi_{12}(\zeta),\Psi_{22}(\zeta),\Psi_{12}^{\prime}(\zeta),\Psi_{22}^{\prime}(\zeta)=O\big(\zeta^{1/4}e^{-2\zeta^{1/2}}\big)\text{ as $\zeta\to\infty$, uniformly for $|\arg\zeta|<\pi$},

\displaystyle\Psi_{12}(\zeta)=O\left(\log\zeta\right)\text{ as $\zeta\to 0$},\qquad\Psi_{22}(\zeta)=O(1)\text{ as $\zeta\to 0$},

\displaystyle\Psi_{12}^{\prime}(\zeta)=O\left(\frac{1}{\zeta}\right)\text{ as $\zeta\to 0$},\qquad\Psi_{22}^{\prime}(\zeta)=O\left(\frac{1}{\zeta}\right)\text{ as $\zeta\to 0$}.

(B.14)

These estimates follow from
[
1
]
formulas (9.6.11), (9.6.13), (9.6.27), (9.7.2), (9.7.4), and the fact that
K_{0}
is a solution to the modified Bessel equation

\displaystyle x^{2}K_{0}^{\prime\prime}(x)+xK_{0}^{\prime}(x)-x^{2}=0.

For clarity, we depict the contours in which
\tilde{\mu}
is given by different expansions, see (

), in Figs.

,

. In all instances where we integrate over the contour

, the integral over

centered at

. This decomposition is shown in Fig.
13
.
